<meta>Shelfmark Clm 849. This page continues a ritual process for summoning spirits to act as teachers. The previous context likely involved preliminary prayers or the preparation of the ritual space.</meta>

...be turned. Then he says: <term>Apolin</term><note>Likely Apollyon or Apollo, often identified in medieval grimoires as a powerful spirit or fallen angel.</note>, **Astaroth**, **Berith**. I, [Name], exorcise and conjure you by the power of Almighty God—who, by your own choice, sent you into the earth, O spirits of the abyss—that you must send to me a certain spirit <margin>E <unclear>u... i... m...</unclear></margin> instructed in the doctrine of all sciences. May he be benevolent, faithful, and calm toward me for the purpose of teaching every science I shall desire, coming in the form of a master so that I may have no fear. I command it: let it be done, let it be done.

† <note>Original: A small sketched Latin cross</note> Likewise, I conjure you by the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, and by these seven names <note>Though the text specifies "seven names," a much longer list of magical names follows, a common occurrence in ritual manuscripts where "seven" may refer to categories or planetary associations.</note> which are understood here, namely: **Dobel**, **Vigel**, **Sabaoth** <note>Hebrew: "Hosts"</note>, **Semon**, **Adonay** <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note>, **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter name of God, YHWH</note>, **Albumayroth**, **Moveth**, **Sadaym**, **Rodobel**, **Domiel**, **Pavabiel**, **Alaciel**, **Noiem**, **Noiam**, **Sobel**. 

Insofar as you yourselves [?] come with me and to me—that is, assign and send to me one of your subordinates who is a master of all sciences and arts, coming in a peaceful human form, shining before me and instructing me with love, in such a way and manner that within the term of 30 days I may be able to obtain such knowledge. [Then] sending him back to his own seat, namely, [you must] where...

<summary>This page contains a conjuration from a Latin grimoire intended to summon a subordinate spirit to serve as a personal tutor. The practitioner seeks to master all "sciences and arts" within a thirty-day period through the instruction of a spirit appearing in a non-threatening human form.</summary>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>Shelfmark Clm 849. This page continues the ritual instructions for summoning a teaching spirit. The previous page concluded with the conjuration of various spirits to assign a master of sciences to the practitioner.</meta>

...the <term>licentiam recedendi</term><note>Latin: "permission to depart." In ritual magic, this is the formal dismissal of spirits to ensure they leave peacefully and do not linger to cause harm.</note>, and this also ought to be said just as many times. When this has been said, put down the sword and wrap it in the aforementioned cloth; make a small bundle, lie down upon it, and sleep for a short while. 

After your sleep, however, rise and dress yourself, because once the bundle is made, a man strips himself, enters the bedchamber, and places the said bundle under his head. 

It must be known, moreover, that once these conjurations have been performed, sleep occurs by divine power; for in that sleep there will appear to you **three greatest kings** with countless servants, knights, and footmen. Among them there is also a certain master who appears, whom those three kings command—for you will see him as a truly skilled man. 

You will see those same kings shining with wondrous beauty, who will speak to you in the said sleep with a living voice, saying: "Behold, we give to you that which you have many times requested." And they say to that master: "Let this man be your disciple, whom you shall teach, as we command, every science or art which he shall wish to hear. Teach and instruct him in such a way that within the term of 30 days, he may surpass all others in whatever science he desired." 

And you will see him respond: "My lords, I will most willingly do that which [you command]..."

<summary>This page describes a dream incubation ritual where the practitioner sleeps on a consecrated sword to receive a vision of three kings who assign a supernatural master to teach them any science within thirty days.</summary>
<keywords>dream incubation, three kings, ritual magic, teaching spirits, occult pedagogy, sword ritual, 30-day mastery</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>Shelfmark Clm 849. This page continues the instructions for the practitioner's interaction with the spirits after the dream incubation. The practitioner has just seen three kings in a dream who have assigned a spirit master to teach them.</meta>

Meanwhile, once these words have been spoken, the kings will depart and the master alone will remain. He will say to you, "Rise, behold your master." Once these words are uttered and you have been awakened, you shall immediately open your eyes and see a certain master, very finely dressed, who will say to you: "Give me the sword <term>ensem</term><note>Latin: *ensem* (accusative of *ensis*), a poetic or formal word for a sword.</note> which you hold beneath your head." 

You should then say: "Behold your disciple, ready to do what pleases you." Nevertheless, you must have a writing tablet <term>pugillarem</term><note>Latin: *pugillarem*. A small set of tablets, often wax-coated, used for taking quick notes.</note> and write down everything he says to you. 

First, you must ask: "O master, what is your name?" He will tell you, and you should write it down. Second, ask from what <term>order</term><note>In medieval magic, spirits were classified into "orders" or hierarchies (like the choirs of angels). Knowing the order helped the practitioner understand the spirit's specific powers and duties.</note> he comes, and write that down as well. 

When these things are finished, he will ask you for the sword. Once he has received it, he will depart, saying: "Wait for me until I return." You must say nothing in response. 

The master will then leave, taking the sword with him. After his departure, you should untie the cloth so that it opens at the bottom. You must then write his name—the one you recorded yourself—within the aforementioned circle. This must be written by you using the <term>aforesaid blood</term><note>Likely referring to blood from a previous sacrifice or a specific animal mentioned earlier in the ritual's preparation, used here as a potent "ink" for binding the spirit.</note>. Once the name is written, wrap the cloth up again and hide it away securely. 

When these tasks are complete, you must dine only on bread and pure water. You are not to leave the chamber on that day. Once you have finished your meal, take the cloth and turn the circle toward <term>Apolin</term><note>A medieval variant of Apollo. In this ritual context, he is treated as a powerful "king" or planetary spirit who presides over the operation.</note> and say as follows:

> O King
> Apolin, great, powerful, and venerable

<summary>This page describes the transition from the ritual dream to a waking vision where the practitioner meets their spirit master, records his name and rank, and performs further consecrations with blood. It concludes with a strict dietary fast and a prayer to the spirit-king Apolin.</summary>
<keywords>spirit master, ritual tools, writing tablet, blood ink, Apolin, ritual fasting, name of power</keywords>

<meta>Shelfmark Clm 849. This page continues the ritual instructions following the practitioner's encounter with a spirit master. Having obtained the spirit's name and order, the practitioner now performs a formal conjuration and creates a physical sigil to bind the master to their service.</meta>

I, your servant <term>famulus</term><note>Latin: *famulus*. A common term in medieval magic for the practitioner, emphasizing a humble or subordinate relationship to the divine or the spirits being commanded.</note>, rightly believing <note>original: *iuta*, likely a scribal error for *iuste* (rightly) or *ita* (thus).</note> and entirely trusting that you are strong and powerful, I ask through your incomprehensible majesty that your servant and subject, [Name], my master, should come to me as quickly as can be done through your virtue and power, which is great and greatest forever and ever. Amen.

And similarly, the verse is said for **Havaloth** by changing the name, and the verse for **Berich** is said in the same way. When these things have been spoken, take some of the aforementioned blood and write your name in the middle of the circle with the aforementioned heart <note>The text specifies using the "aforesaid heart" (*dicto corde*), likely referring to the heart of the animal whose blood was harvested earlier, used here as a writing instrument or ritual object.</note> as it appears below. Then, write with the aforementioned heart those names in their individual parts, as it appears here:

->**THE CIRCLE OF THE MASTER**<-

| **Belial** | **East** <note>original: *Oriens*</note> | **Satan** |
| :--- | :---: | ---: |
| **North** <note>original: *Septentrio*</note> | **Apolyn** <br/> **Beruthinus** <br/><br/> Name of the master of such an order. <br/> Name of the disciple. <br/><br/> **Aendor** <br/> **Havalot** | **South** <note>original: *Meridies*</note> |
| **Lucifer** | **West** <note>original: *Occidens*</note> | **Beelzebub** |

<note>The diagram shows a protective square containing concentric circles. The corners are anchored by the four great princes of the infernal hierarchy (Belial, Satan, Lucifer, and Beelzebub), while the cardinal directions align the ritual space with the cosmos. The names of the specific spirits (Apolyn, Beruthinus, etc.) act as intermediaries, with the names of the spirit master and the human student placed at the very center of power.</note>

<summary>This page provides the specific prayer and diagram needed to summon and bind the spirit master to the practitioner. It details the use of blood and a ritual heart to transcribe the names of powerful demons and the practitioner's own name within a protective circle.</summary>
<keywords>conjuration, magic circle, Belial, Satan, Lucifer, Beelzebub, Havaloth, ritual blood, spirit master, necromancy</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>Shelfmark Clm 849, page 36. This page continues the ritual sequence from page 35, where the practitioner has placed a consecrated cloth under their pillow to summon a spirit master in a dream. This section describes the appearance of the master, the duration of the instruction (thirty days), and the method for dismissing and recalling the spirit using the ritual cloth.</meta>

Indeed, he gave my master to me for the sake of memory, that you might receive your kings as you would wish, in a kind form; I have been given such <term>knowledge</term><note>Latin: *scientiam*. Referring again to the specific magical art or branch of hidden wisdom the practitioner seeks to acquire.</note> that I now surpass all mortals by learning it with great joy, without any labor, and with all weariness cast aside. Come therefore from your part, you who are better, who reign for infinite ages of ages. Amen. So be it, so be it, so be it.

With these things having been said, a man will then appear; you will see the master [coming] from the West <note>original: *occidentem*. Directions often held ritual significance in medieval magic; the West was frequently associated with the sunset and the spirit world.</note> with many disciples. You shall ask him to command them all to go away, and they shall immediately depart. Since the master himself will ask what knowledge you desire to hear, you shall say, "Such [and such]." And then many <note>original: *multi*. Likely referring to the departing disciples or other spirit voices.</note> will cry out that you should nevertheless learn whatever he tells you and commit it to memory. 

And you shall learn all the knowledge which you wish to learn within thirty days. And whenever you wish to leave the <term>chamber</term><note>Latin: *camera*. The private room where the ritual is performed.</note>, fold the cloth and put it away <note>original: *retunde*. Literally "beat back" or "blunt," but here used as a ritual action to signal the end of the session.</note>, and he will immediately depart. And whenever you wish him to come, open the cloth; suddenly he will appear there, continuing the lessons through the limit of thirty days. 

[To this] most excellent teacher in knowledge, have him give you your sword and tell him to go and depart in peace. You must speak again for another knowledge now to be invoked, who will tell you that he is ready at your pleasure. 

The chapter on the second knowledge is finished.

<summary>This page concludes the instructions for summoning a dream-teacher, specifying that the spirit appears from the West with disciples and provides thirty days of effortless instruction. --- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the previous instructions on acquiring wisdom from a master spirit to a specific ritual of "malefic" magic—specifically, how to strip an enemy of their reason and intelligence. The hand remains the same, though the subject matter shifts from self-improvement to harming another.</meta>

To this end, therefore, so that you may know how you can take [wisdom] away from someone <note>original: *à baliquo*, likely a scribal error for *ab aliquo* (from someone).</note>, listen diligently. For you ought first, on a Saturday during the waning moon <note>The waning moon was traditionally associated with destructive or diminishing magic.</note> at the first hour, to come in your mind to that place you desire, and before him let [the following] be said in a clear voice. If you have submitted Amael before your circle, let him dissolve and abolish all wisdom, sense, and discernment of thought. I conjure you, Amael, by all the princes and elders, and by that which you wish to do, that you must appear in such <unclear>office</unclear> and remain as long as it pleases me, and to darken him and [cause] him to lose everything he knows; otherwise, I will send you into the depths of the sea, from which you shall not depart forever and ever. With these things said, depart and speak forth until it was evening.

Once this is done, go to his door <note>original: *hostium*, likely a misspelling of *ostium* (door/entrance).</note> and take from the wood of that door enough that you can make a pen <term>calamus</term><note>A reed pen or writing implement.</note> from it. And go home and make a pen from it, and anoint it with the blood of a cat <term>murilegi</term><note>Latin: *murilegus*, literally "mouse-gatherer," a common term for a cat.</note>. With that pen write: "Amael." Having taken away his wisdom, bring it about that you are present in the midst and in the other senses of such a person, and [this] must always be done. By which [act] he is driven mad <note>original: *dementam*. The ritual aims to induce insanity or cognitive loss.</note>. For which invocation a circle should be written on the said cloth with the said blood, as is evident here above, where it is written as follows: The name of him whom you wish to deprive [of sense] is placed in the middle of the circle with this name "Amael," to whom it must be said thus: "I conjure you, demons..."

<summary>This page describes a ritual to strip a target of their intelligence and sanity using the spirit Amael. It involves specific timing (waning moon on a Saturday), the use of wood from the target's door to make a pen, and writing with cat's blood.</summary>
<keywords>Amael, waning moon, Saturday, cat's blood, madness, binding ritual, sympathetic magic, necromancy</keywords>

...the evil ones <term>malignos</term><note>Referring to malevolent spirits or demons.</note>, namely Mecath, Pinen, Ocel, Triyboy, Noriyoth, Belfarith, Camoy, Astaroth, Sobvonoy, and Ismael. By the undivided Trinity, namely the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit proceeding from both <note>This refers to the *Filioque* clause, a theological statement that the Spirit comes from both the Father and the Son.</note>, and by the trembling and fearful day of judgment, and by all things that have been, and are, and shall be—and just as you are depicted in this circle—it [the ritual] overcomes such a person so that they may come to this state and be so affected. <meta>This page continues instructions for a ritual of binding or malevolent magic, specifically focusing on the manipulation of a wax figure and a candle to consume an individual's power or vitality.</meta>

...may bring about. And you, <term>mivael</term> <note>original: "mivael"; likely a specific name for the wax image or a spirit associated with it</note> made of his wax, you shall never depart, remaining with me day and night, nor will I command you to leave him. When these things have been said, return again in the same night to your house with a hard cloth and with a certain knife and with the wet <unclear>things</unclear> you have seen, all the way to his door. Bend yourself to the ground, <unclear>migat</unclear> itself <unclear>i[...]</unclear> in the manner of a channel, making a pit under the threshold of the house as far as the boundary marker. Then say: "I bury such an image of the inscribed and summoned demons, so that just as these demons are summoned, all your power <note>original: "virtus"; here referring to a person's vital force or efficacy</note> may be buried." And when the said circle has been covered with earth, return again to the house.

And make a candle in which all the things are written, held with a <term>mavaculo</term> <note>A ritual tool, likely a stylus or a specialized candle-holder</note>. You must make it from wax purchased for the first time, [inscribing] the names and the scorn for him with a needle likewise [newly] purchased. When this is done, light it and say thus: "Just as this candle is made in scorn and is bound and consumed, so may all his power existing within him turn into the damned weight and depravity of the demons written on this candle. And just as you demons written here see, so may you have no rest until you have brought this to its effect." Once these things have been said, <unclear>gula</unclear> <note>Likely a typo for a ritual action, perhaps involving the throat (gula) or a command to "swallow" the light</note> and extinguish the said candle, saying these words: "Just as this candle is extinguished, so all power remaining in such a one shall be utterly consumed." Then light it again, saying: "Just as this..."

<summary>This page details a ritual involving the burial of a demonic wax image under a target's threshold and the burning of a specialized candle to drain the target's personal power. It emphasizes the use of "virgin" materials (wax and a needle purchased for the first time) to ensure the spell's efficacy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the ritual involving the wax image and candles. It describes the mental effects of the spell on the victim over a seven-day period and then provides the specific counter-ritual to release the victim from the binding, involving the use of olive wood, verbena, and the unburying of the ritual cloth.</meta>

Two candles <unclear>shown</unclear> <note>original: "os ta"; possibly "ostensa" (shown) or a specific ritual instruction</note> from the next morning until the limit of seven days. When these have been reckoned from the first day or night, indeed, on the third day you will see him <term>demented</term> <note>original: "dementem"; out of his mind or delirious</note> in such a way that everyone will marvel. It is a wondrous thing because it happened, and because he will not believe himself to have any defect, but thinks all other minds are hollow. Therefore, hold this <term>experiment</term> <note>original: "experientiam"; in magical texts, an 'experiment' refers to a specific tested ritual or recipe</note> exactly as it is, because it is of great power.

When you wish to release him from what was commanded, it can best be done on <term>Thursday</term> <note>original: "die jovis"; the Day of Jupiter, often associated with justice, release, and relief</note> in the hour of night. Go to his door where the <term>cloth</term> <note>original: "pannu"; refers to the cloth used to wrap the image or buried under the threshold on the previous page</note> is <unclear>buried</unclear> <note>original: "sapallitur"; likely a corruption of "sepelitur" (buried)</note>. While it is being <unclear>unburied</unclear>, it must be placed by itself in a certain vessel so that it does not rot. Thus the prayer for liberating him is performed; while unwrapping the cloth, you shall say thus:

-> Anidali orsoth. <-
-> Amen. ocel. tuay moriyoth Welfarion. <-
-> Candy. Arawoth. obwonoy. fismael. <-
-> I, <insert>name</insert>, absolve you so that you may go at your pleasure <-
-> and leave <insert>name</insert> in peace forever. <-

When these things have been said, make a gift of the cloth itself. Once a fire of olive wood has been lit, and <term>spirits</term> <note>original: "spiritula"; likely referring to aromatic resins or incense</note> and the herb <term>verbena</term> <note>A common plant in medieval magic used for protection and healing</note> have been added, the said cloth shall be cast into the fire, saying: "Just as this fire consumes this cloth, so may every spirit that <margin>☩</margin> <unclear>afflicted</unclear> <note>original: "pavit"; potentially "struck" or "terrified"</note> such a person be quickly consumed." 

When the fire is burned out, throw the said powder into running water, and all his <term>destruction</term> <note>original: "destructa"; the harmful effects of the spell</note> will be undone. He will know himself as he was before, but because it was at first only a temporary <unclear>affliction</unclear>, he will believe himself to have had a sickness, and will think it happened by that occasion, and so forth.

<summary>This page describes the psychological symptoms of the binding spell—causing the victim to believe others are "hollow" while they themselves are delirious—and provides a detailed reversal ritual involving unburying a cloth and burning it with olive wood and verbena on a Thursday.</summary>
<keywords>binding reversal, Thursday ritual, olive wood, verbena, madness, mental symptoms, absolution, ritual cleansing</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general binding and release rituals of the previous page to a specific "experiment" for gaining the love of a woman. It introduces new ritual components including animal sacrifice and the use of specific spirit names.</meta>

<margin>
In the name of Christ, the beginning [...] through [...] <unclear>through</unclear> <unclear>arrived</unclear> [...] with the greatest diligence of a long time and from danger [...] f [...] and [...]

<margin>a</margin>

**W**hen you wish to have the love of any woman, whether the way be far or near, and whether she be noble or poor, on whatever day or night, whether in the increase <note>original: "augmento"; likely referring to the waxing moon</note> or in the waning of friendship, first you must have a certain dove that is entirely white, and <term>parchment</term> <note>original: "carta"</note> made from a <term>female dog</term> <note>original: "cane feta"; a dog that has given birth or is brooding</note> while she is in heat <note>original: "dum est in amore"</note>, from which the earth is lightest; and one ought to know that the aforementioned parchment is most powerful for the love of women according to the Master. You must also have an <term>eagle's quill</term> <note>original: "calamum aquile"</note>, and in a hidden place take the said dove and with your teeth bite its heart-veins so that the heart comes out. And with the eagle's quill, write on the said parchment with the said blood the name of her whom you desire, according to an image of a naked woman, drawn as best you can, saying the following formula: **N.** <note>A placeholder for the target's name</note>, daughter of such a person, whom I desire to have among the six <term>hot spirits</term> <note>original: "spirituum calidorum"; in medieval magic, 'hot' spirits were associated with passion, lust, and rapid action</note>, namely: **Tubal, Sathan, Fauces, Cupido, Asalion, Duliaco.** May she love me above all living things of this world. When this is done, write her name upon her forehead and this name **Tubal**, saying: "You are **N.**, daughter of such a person, held and disposed to my will, and you are **Tubal**; upon her forehead I hold you, binding the senses of her head to me, even as she desires." Afterwards, write on her right arm **Sathan** and in...

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific "experiment" for binding the love of a woman. It details the precise locations on the parchment image where the names of the "hot spirits" and the practitioner must be written, accompanied by specific incantations to compel the woman's desire and ensure she dreams of the practitioner.</meta>

...on the left [write] **Fauces**. Having written these things, speak thus: "Just as you, **Sathan**, and you, **Fauces**, who are written upon this image, become [bound] in the name of such a woman, so that you may continually afflict her in her innermost parts <note>original: "vachia"; likely a corruption of "vacia" or "ventre," referring to the womb or the internal void of longing</note> so that she refuses to do anything, but rather desires to embrace me."

Once this is done, again write your own name near the heart of the image, saying: "Just as I, **N.** <note>The practitioner's name</note>, am written in the heart of this image, so may such a woman <note>original: "talis N."</note> have me in her heart day and night."

Once this is done, write upon the face of the image this name: **Cupido**, saying thus: "Just as you, **Cupido**, are upon the face of this image, so may you always remain upon the face of such a woman, inflaming her so that she despises all the men of this world and desires me alone, and let the fire of my love touch and inflame her."

Once this is done, write on the legs: on the right, **Asalion**; on the left, **Juliatus**. Having written these, say: "Just as you, **Asalion**, and you, **Juliatus**, who are written on this image, so may you sit upon the legs of such a woman, afflicting her soul because of my vehement love, so that she does not wish to go anywhere, nor desire to live for anything, except for this <note>meaning, to come to the practitioner</note>."

When these things have been said, take the image itself with both hands and, on bended knees, say thus: "I have drawn the heart and mind of such a woman through this image, and I provoke in her a strong love, so that she may love me, desire me, and long for me, and also look upon me in her sleep all through the night; through our Lord Jesus Christ, who lives..." <note>The text ends with a common Christian liturgical formula, demonstrating how medieval magic often blended demonic invocations with Christian prayers.</note>

...and reigns and commands forever. > I conjure you, all demons written upon this image, by your lords whom you are bound to obey: by **Obedon**, **Badalam**, and **Levith**, so that such a woman <note>original: "talem"; referring to the target of the spell</note>, whose image is here depicted by name, may be kindled and bound in love for me, so that day and night she thinks of me and hopes for me, until they <note>referring to the spirits</note> effectively fulfill my will; and just as you are written and fixed upon this image, so may they be present within her until I do with her as I wish.

> "I cannot go on without you."

And then you may hold her as a constant companion, and she will do her master's <note>referring to the practitioner</note> will. Indeed, what is even more wonderful about this is that she will have a sign as if she had seen your very face through the creation of this image.

Because when the use of the ring has been skillfully performed, you will hear a certain voice saying: "Behold, we are here." When these things have been said, let them suddenly withdraw. She shall be carried before that very gate without any injury.

She will hold out her hand to you, and you shall draw her inside. Although she may be somewhat frantic <note>original: "fetonica"; likely a corruption of 'phrenetica,' implying the woman is in a trance or an agitated state due to the compulsion.</note>, she nonetheless comes most willingly.

**Concerning obtaining the love of a woman**
I wish to bequeath to you a most pleasing art for obtaining dignity and honor, peace, and the greatest incorruptible affection from a king, or from prelates or lords, and generally from any man you might wish. First, therefore, obtain two soft stones, which you shall rub against one another until their surfaces are flat and match perfectly. This being done, carve into one the image of the person you desire, beginning from the head and continuing down to the feet, first making the front part and then carving the back; also carve a crown upon the head if he is a king, and so on.

Before hell is even reached, let it be so that one and the other are in flames, and that one cannot bear to see the other; rather, let them at some time be afflicted by unchangeable hatred and wars, and let all love, affection, brotherhood, and union be removed from them, leading to enmity and the greatest hatred as quickly as can be.

When these things are finished, he ought to continue by fumigating <note>using incense or sulfurous smoke</note> them always, repeated at night; and let him strike two stones together and sometimes throw one over the other, saying thus:

> I do not strike these stones; rather, I strike such and such a person [and such and such a person], whose names are carved here, so that one may continually afflict the other, and from now on they may nourish themselves toward enmity with unmitigable hatred.

And you shall do this every night and every day, three times for the following days, and you will see in part or hear that they are greatly afflicted and will hate each other so much that one cannot stand to see the other.

If, however, you wish to separate them entirely and desire to segregate one from the other so that one flees from the other, do it in this manner: Arise on the day of Saturday <note>the day of Saturn, associated with endings and boundaries</note> before sunrise, with the moon waning, and especially when it is in <term>combustion</term> <note>an astrological state where a planet is so close to the sun that its power is 'burnt up' or hidden</note>; and then perform this toward the sunrise. <meta>This page continues the instructions for a ritual of discord involving enchanted stones. ...Prepare and foster their enmity by rubbing and striking them against each other, one over the other, while saying: "I do not hide these stones, but may you bury all the [remains] of the earth <note>original: "deis terr sepelias," likely referring to burying the ritual objects in the deep earth or 'gods of the earth'</note> whom you wish." Then withdraw and go toward the West; there, make a pit and bury the other stones, saying as follows: 

"Just as I have separated these stones, so let such and such a person be separated from the other, and let them be set against one another just as these stones which are buried." 

Withdraw, and you will see them disjoined and separated from one another. 

This proven ritual <term>experimentum</term> <note>In medieval grimoires, an 'experimentum' refers to a tested or proven magical operation</note> must be kept secret because an infallible power exists within it; for no remedy can be found to rejoin them if they bite and hate each other. 

When, however, you wish them to remain in their former grace, the aforementioned stones must be dug up and placed in a furnace. When they are well-baked, let them be ground very finely; mix them with water into a paste and allow it to dry. Once dried, throw it into a flowing river <note>original: "fluie aq," likely *fluviis aquae*</note>, saying: "Let all enmity and anger that was between such and such a person be taken away, and let them return to their former love through the mercy of the pious God who does not look upon the wickedness of sinners. Amen." 

And know that by these prayers, all anger is lifted and the former peace returns.

### To Have a Ship to Travel Anywhere

So that wherever you may be, you are able to have a ship in which you, with whomsoever you wish, may go wherever you favor. They are to be held on **Monday** <note>original: "die lune." In planetary magic, Monday is associated with the Moon, which governs water, travel, and illusions.</note>. On that same Monday, when it is <unclear>set</unclear> for the favor of worship under a serene sky, go outside to a remote place and perform the whole matter by hand...

<meta>This page continues the ritual instructions for the magical bridle. They will remain until you shake the bridle, and suddenly a **blackened horse** <note>original: "equus nigervatū," likely a corruption of *nigrefactus* or a specific magical term for a dark-colored spirit horse.</note> will come to that place. Such bridles are made very thin, so that they can be carried in a small space, and they can never be broken or rot.

When you wish to infuse <note>original: "infundere," implying the pouring of life or spirit into a form.</note> someone who is dead so that they appear as if they were alive, such an order must be followed. ## The Art of Invisibility

I also treat the art of human invisibility, which is almost unknown to everyone. Since, therefore, you will be able to act among all people as reasonably as if you were not visible, thus you are considered imperceptible. First, during a new moon on a Wednesday <note>original: "die mercurii." In Western magic, Wednesday—the day of Mercury—is considered the most auspicious time for spells involving speed, communication, and deception or invisibility.</note> at the first hour of the day, take a partridge, and having shaved your head and beard and dressed in white, leave the town for a hidden, clear-scented place on level ground. When you have prepared yourself, make a circle as it appears here, writing these names together with everything appearing there:

When the image has been formed in this way, you must **Christianize** <note>original: "xpianizes." When you do this, submerge it in the earth and say the name by which it is called, as it is seen: "**N.**" And you ought to say:

->**I baptize you, N., in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.**<-

And afterwards, place the image in a new and clean cloth, leaving it from the **hour of the Sun** until the **hour of Mars**. Then, you shall perform this conjuration under a fruit-bearing tree over glowing coals. And turn yourself toward the East and say:

"I conjure you, **N.**, by your head, your hair, your eyes, your ears, your cheeks. I conjure you, **N.**, by your brain. I conjure you, **N.**, by your <unclear>vitality</ununclear> <note>original: "trunitatem." This is likely a scribal error for "trusitatem" or "trutinam," perhaps referring to the throat or the balance of the person's life force.</note>..."

**To have a horse to go wherever you wish** <note>original: "Ut equū hēas ad eundū quo vis." This is a common type of medieval spell for "fast travel," often involving the summoning of a spirit in the form of a horse or a magical means of transportation.</note>
Look with your face toward the east, with bended knees and joined hands—and if you can, in a cave <note>original: "cauta," likely a shorthand for "caverna."</note> in the open air <note>original: "per dio," referring to being under the sky or in the light of day.</note>—with great confidence and very firm hope, you shall say:
O Most High and most kind King Almighty, hear my prayer and let my cry come unto thee. Let thine ears be attentive to the vows of me, your suppliant. By the most noble seat of your majesty, I invoke and implore <note>original: "adiuvo," literally "I help," but used here in the sense of "I call upon" or "I adjure."</note> you by that Lord whom you love and trust with all your being.
By **Adonai** <note>Hebrew: "Lord."</note>, by the lawful law, and by the holy faith, and by the charity of charities, O Almighty; by **On**, **El**, **Ely** <note>Transliterated Hebrew names for God: "On" (often associated with light/existence), "El" (God), and "Ely" (a variation of Eli, "My God").</note>, and by the **Tetragrammaton** <note>term: "Tetragrammaton" (the four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, considered too sacred to pronounce).</note>, which is the Most High name; by the place where you sit upon the throne of your great nobility: send to me the holy guiding spirit <note>original: "sp[iritu]m s[an]c[tu]m regēū." While "spiritum sanctum" usually means Holy Spirit, in this magical context it refers to a "holy" or "sanctified" spirit-servant sent by God.</note> so that with him I may be able to fulfill what I desire to accomplish. O powerful Lord, send him so that in this hour, by your great power, he may be obedient and lawful to me in all things and through all things. And therefore, I invoke, adore, and praise your eternal name, O Lord, who rising blesses all, through the most glorious, for infinite ages of ages. Amen.

When this has been said, you should know to say boldly: "I wish now to go to such a place and country." Whether you wish to be upon a horse, or whether sleeping or waking, let him come with horses. I say, I conjure you by the Lord who created heaven and earth, the sea, and all things that are in them, both visible and invisible, that without [frightening] presence, fear, loss, or any danger or distress to my heart... <note>The text cuts off here, but it is a standard protection clause to ensure the spirit appears in a non-threatening form and does not harm the practitioner.</note>

# To find in dreams whatever you wish
Write these names on virgin parchment <note>original: "carta virginea," referring to parchment made from the skin of a newborn or unborn animal that has never been used for any other purpose</note> with those names [of the spirits] in this manner, as will be shown in the figure later. Afterwards, place it under your right ear when you go to sleep, and you will see whatever you wish regarding the past, the present, and the future. This is from God.

### Here begins the Mirror of Floron

Have a mirror made of **pure steel** <note>original: "puro calibe"; steel was the preferred material for magical mirrors in the Middle Ages because of its reflective quality and its association with the planetary power of Mars</note> to the measurement of **one palm** in the round. Let it have a **handle** for holding it, and let it be polished and bright like a sword. Let [the mirror] always be in the mouth of Floron <note>This likely refers to the spirit Floron appearing to the magician, or perhaps the mirror is physically set into a frame shaped like a head or mouth</note>. In the circumference of the mirror itself, on the **other side** (the non-polished side), let these **ten names** be written along with these **ten characters** <note>original: "carateribus"; these are "sigils" or occult symbols used to bind the power of spirits to the object</note>. And let the name **Floron** be written there in the middle. After it has been made in a **consecrated place**, let it be anointed with **pure balsam** on the non-polished side, and let it be fumigated <note>to "fumigate" is to pass the object through the smoke of specific herbs or resins to purify or empower it</note>, namely:

Strive to find a black cat born in the month of March and eviscerate it for the heart; then immediately cut out the heart with a knife that is made of <margin>of</margin> iron from the vein <note>original: "de vene ferro." And note that in taking it, you should then hold the mirror before you, looking at one grain after another in the mirror; and place all the grains in your mouth under your tongue, at least according to your desire. And regarding that grain which you place in your mouth: whichever grain is placed such that you are unable to see yourself at all in the mirror <note>The mirror serves as the test for the invisibility spell; if the reflection disappears, the seed is magically active</note>, know that it is good, and see etc / etc.

### To find a theft

Take a virgin boy born of a legitimate marriage <note>original: "legittio thoro," literally 'legitimate bed'; the ritual purity of the child was believed to make them more sensitive to spiritual visions</note> at whatever hour you wish before noon. You shall clean the nail of the thumb of his left hand well with a knife; then you shall bind a small slip of paper <note>original: "cedula"</note> with the names noted below upon that same finger under the nail. And these are the names:

> Egypna. benoham. beante or beannie.
> recuavessym. Alvadessym. Ebemidyra.
> facolmie. dysi. medum. allyea here
> sim. Egypna. benoham. Kallam. kywa
> hiae. bohodi. hohada. Annah. yohanna.
> dyeves. met alitays. flvegeveal. agevba
> allibcio. halla. <note>These are "words of power" or "barbarous names"—strings of sounds intended to compel spiritual forces through their ancient or exotic resonance</note>

With this done, the writing was not <unclear>seen</unclear>.

may you receive such virtue that whoever is bound

wherever the binder wishes, let it suffice." **Here ends

### Ritual for the Young Medium

...devoutly, both by the name of the Prince of the highest God and by the crown of His head, and by that reverence which you owe to Him, applied for the good of the world and with the opportunity set before you; and in this tongue through which you may appear to us without doubt and without deceit, that which we wish to know from you, manifest truthfully. **Let it be, let it be, let it be.**

**I conjure you**, young maiden <note>original: "virgo iuvenis." In medieval magic, this often refers to a young, "pure" child—boy or girl—acting as a medium to look into a mirror or water</note>, by that name of God, **Orion**, **Sabaoth** <note>original Hebrew: "Lord of Hosts"</note>, **Adonay** <note>original Hebrew: "Lord"</note>, so that you do not have the power of lying or concealing regarding whatever I shall ask you. 

**I conjure you**, young maiden, by your God **Alpha and Omega** <note>the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, representing the beginning and the end</note>, who is the first and the last, that you tell me the truth about this matter of which I inquire of you. 

**I conjure you**, maiden, by the baptism which you received in the name of the holy and individual Trinity, by the love and power by which God ruled you through water and the Holy Spirit, so that you may have neither power nor permission to hide or lie about whatever you shall see. 

**I conjure you**, young maiden, by the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, by the holy name of God **Eleyon** <note>original Greek/Hebrew: "Most High"</note>, by the holy **Tetragrammaton** <note>The sacred four-letter name of God, YHWH</note>, by the holy names of God: **On**, **Ely**, **Aloy**, **Aloe** <note>Vocalizations of various Hebrew names for God like El or Elohi</note>, so that you do not have permission or power to hide yourself, wherever you are released. 

**I conjure you**, young maiden, by baptism and by the sacraments of holy faith, by the love and power by which He ruled you... so let it be that as quickly and without delay, openly and briefly in this tongue, you bring forth the truth.

<summary>This page contains the formal "conjurations" or binding oaths spoken over a young medium to ensure they provide a truthful prophetic vision. It blends orthodox Christian references to baptism and the Trinity with powerful Hebrew and Greek names for God common in medieval magic.</summary>

<keywords>conjuration, scrying, medium, ritual magic, names of God, baptism, divination, truth-telling</keywords>

# Ritual for Scrying by the Fingernail

...to manifest to us another person, [let the] child [do so], who may appear through the Son and the Holy Spirit, through the names of God: **Adonai**, **Eloy**, **Elohe** <note>Hebrew names for God signifying "Lord" and "Divine One"</note>, and the **Tetragrammaton** <note>the sacred four-letter name of God, YHWH</note>, and through the names of our Lord Jesus Christ: **Eloy, Eloy, Eloe** <note>referencing Christ’s cry from the cross, "My God, my God..."</note>, and through the baptism which he received in the name of the Father and of the indivisible Trinity, and through the love of God, **Alpha and Omega** <note>the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet</note>, who is the beginning and the end.

That you may appear in the fingernail <note>original: "ungula." This refers to onychomancy, a divination technique where a child medium stares at a polished, oiled, or waxed fingernail to see visions</note> in such a way that it should brighten. If it does not, then ask the boy if it is truly clear; and if he says "no" beforehand, then read one **absolution** <note>a prayer or formal command of release/cleansing</note> and repeat the process if it is necessary. If he says that it is clear, ask him if he sees anything. If he says that he sees a man, let the master deliver this command according to what he sees in the mother tongue <note>original: "materna lingua." The text instructs the practitioner to switch from ritual Latin to the medium’s daily language (likely German) for the actual command</note>, as follows: 

"You who are before me, I adjure you by the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, and by Holy **Mary** and her virginity, by Saint **John** and his virginity, by Saint **Catherine** and her virginity, and all sixteen virgins, and by my own virginity <note>the spiritual purity of the young medium was believed to be the "battery" for the vision</note>, that you go quickly to your king and make him come before me, so that I may see him clearly and understand him."

And afterward, ask the boy if the king has come. If not, let the boy take the oath as before. If he has come, let the boy tell the king to descend from his horse and have a chair <note>original: "cathedram," usually a high-backed seat of authority</note> brought for him to sit upon. Then let the boy ask the king if he wishes to eat. If he says "no," then let the boy ask for what he [the master] wanted to know. If he says "yes," let the boy tell the king that he must first send [his attendants] away.

<summary>This page describes a ritual of onychomancy, where a child medium scries into a fingernail to summon a spiritual "king." It provides the specific commands—to be spoken in the vernacular—to compel the spirit to appear, dismount his horse, and answer questions.</summary>
<keywords>onychomancy, scrying, fingernail divination, medium, King, Adonai, Tetragrammaton, Saint Catherine, Saint John, ritual purity</keywords>

# How to find the certain truth concerning a doubtful matter

Moreover, to find the certain truth that must be held regarding a doubtful matter, take the right shoulder blade <note>original: "spatulam." The use of a shoulder blade bone for divination is known as scapulimancy.</note> of a heifer, anointed with olive oil. Place this upon the handle of a knife in the presence of a virgin boy. Cedon, Zephara, Elohim, Elohay <note>original: "eloym. eloyay." I conjure you by the three kings—Caspar, Balthasar, [and] Melchior—to whom the star appeared through the power of our Lord Jesus Christ so that He might manifest His truth; [I conjure you] so that you may appear in this fingernail of the boy and manifest to us, without deceit and without doubt, what we wish to inquire of you.

I conjure you, you who are called, by the three youths—Shadrach, Meshach, [and] Abednego <note>original: "sydrac. misac. abdenago." These figures from the Biblical Book of Daniel were famously protected from fire, making them popular in rituals involving candles, smoke, or light.</note>—so that you may appear in the fingernail of this boy, yet without [causing] fear in him, so that you do not cause terror or injury; and that you make us certain concerning whatever things we desire. 

I conjure you, you who are called, by the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit and by His measurement <note>original: "dyametria." This likely refers to the divine proportions or the "geometry" of the cosmos through which God governs the world.</note> and power; by the <term>Cherubim and Seraphim</term> <note>The highest orders of angels in the celestial hierarchy.</note>, by the Thrones and Dominations, by the Principalities and Powers and Virtues, by the Angels and Archangels; by the Patriarchs and Prophets, by the Apostles, by the Confessors, by the Martyrs, by the Monks, by the Virgins and Widows; by all the Saints and the elect of God; by the nine orders of angels; by the holy birth of the mother of our Lord Jesus Christ, [and] by her virginity...

### Here begins the prologue of the book of consecrations

<image-desc>A large decorative Gothic initial 'I' featuring a vertical stem with a central ornate knot-like flourish and scrollwork extending into the left margin.</image-desc> Here begins the prologue <note>original: "prologus"</note> of the book of consecrations <note>original: "libri consecrationum"</note>, stating what it demonstrates, for what purpose it was invented, and how it results in success. Whoever wishes to carry out any work they have begun should look ahead to the essence of what must be provided for themselves, taking total care that they do not work in vain; and specifically in this work, in other suitable places, as something proper, most praiseworthy, and most certain. Because it is preserved and confirmed in the name of God, no operator <term>operator: the practitioner or ritualist performing the magic</term> can be deceived, provided they have hope and good faith in the Lord Jesus Christ, from whom all good things proceed. For he who does not firmly and faithfully believe cannot be saved; thus, it is necessary for every operator to believe faithfully so as not to be deceived. Know therefore, thirdly, that this work was invented for the purpose of removing [failures] of certain experiments <note>original: "experimentorum"; in this context, "experiments" refers to specific magical recipes or ritual procedures</note>. Namely, so that through the power and invocation of the holy and ineffable names of God, they might obtain a wondrous power and potent effect, which they ought to possess, as will be explained below. When any experiment has lost its power and potency, it can—in the manner that will be described later—recover them. Note that there are many who seek many great things, and they even possess writings and believe...

<summary>This page serves as the formal prologue to the "Book of Consecrations," outlining the spiritual necessity of faith in Jesus Christ for the success of magical operations. It explains that the book was designed to "repair" or reactivate magical rituals (experiments) that have lost their potency through the use of divine names.</summary>
<keywords>prologue, consecration, operator, faith, experiments, divine names, ritual failure, Jesus Christ</keywords>

Let it be hidden from all so that no one may be present while, at the appropriate time, [the operator] sprinkles the place with holy water <term>aqua benedicta: water sanctified by a priest for religious use</term> where he places the book itself. He should bind the book with a priest’s cincture <term>cingulo: a liturgical belt, usually a cord</term> and a dedicated stole <term>stola: a vestment representing the yoke of Christ</term>, tying it in the shape of a cross. 

And then, as much as is necessary, and with bent knees toward the East, he should say the seven psalms <note>original: ".vij. p̄s̄"; refers to the Seven Penitential Psalms, such as Psalm 51, used for purification</note> along with the litany <term>letania: a form of prayer consisting of a series of petitions</term> and the following prayer. He must sprinkle the aforementioned book with humble devotion and declare with his whole heart that Almighty God, in His mercy and goodness, might sanctify, bless, and consecrate the book itself, which is inscribed with His most holy names. This is done so that the book may obtain the power it is meant to hold, and that it may be effective for performing spiritual virtues and for all invocations and conjurations of spirits <note>original: "p̄p̄or"; likely an abbreviation for spirituum</note>, and likewise for all other experiments <term>experimenta: the technical term for specific magical operations or ritual procedures</term>. 

And similarly, this book will have no value for its possessor unless it is consecrated anew by him through the litany or a similar sprinkling, saying this prayer humbly and devoutly (following the holy and mindful principles).

Note indeed the blessing <note>original: "nō. eñ. b⁹" - likely "nota enim benedictio."</note> which was in this psalm, and it pleased me; and <unclear>concede that</unclear> the spirit <note>original: "d f'" - likely "dei famulus" or "daemon factor."</note> may be under my power, and that you may grant him similar power for consecrating the book and the experiments, and all whatever he wished. Go around the graveyard <term>cimiterium: a cemetery or churchyard, often used in medieval magic as a site for gathering materials or performing rituals due to its connection with the spirit world.</term> and collect as many stones as there are words in the psalm *Have mercy on me, O God* <note>original: "miserere mei deus" — the opening of Psalm 51, a staple of Catholic liturgy.</note>. When the spirit <note>original: "spᵗ"</note> comes, say: "If you were willing, you have forsaken the God who begot you and forgotten God your creator." <note>A paraphrase of Deuteronomy 32:18.</note> To which the spirit says: "It is one." <meta>This page continues the catalog of spirits, following the descriptions of Porson, Alygor, and Orobas. # Volach
He is a great **president** <term>President: In grimoires, this is a specific rank in the infernal hierarchy, often overseeing a large number of spirits.</term> and appears like a boy with wings like an angel, riding upon a two-headed dragon. He gives fully true answers concerning hidden treasures and where they may be seen. He also has power over serpents, and can deliver any serpent into the hands of the **exorcist** <note>original: "exorcistae" — in this context, the ritual magician or practitioner summoning the spirit.</note> without their guardians. He has 27 legions under him.

---

# Alaneuon
He is a strong and powerful **duke** and appears in a very beautiful likeness, crowned among leaders <note>original: "inter ductili coronati" — this may also imply "crowned in wrought metal."</note>. He sings like a crow over a ring. He gives fully true answers regarding things past, present, and future, and concerning hidden treasures and the places where they may be seen. He grants favor and the love of women, especially young girls. He has 27 legions under him.

---

# Vuerrías
A great and strong **marquess** reigning and commanding in Africa. He bestows and teaches to the fullest extent grammar, logic, and rhetoric <note>The "Trivium," the foundational subjects of a classical medieval education.</note>. Regarding inheritance <note>original: "heretate" — likely a variant of *haereditate*.</note>, he reveals the locations of treasures, rivers, and hidden things. He also enables a man to cross the sea and rivers with the swiftest speed, and to do so without fear. He appears in the form of a soldier, riding upon an excellent black horse. He has 37 legions under him.

---

...makes a man invisible <note>original: "inuibilem" — likely a variant of *invisibilem*.</note>, or makes him skilled and renowned to masters; and in other liberal arts he provides the best friendships and the favor of great men and princes. He also invisibly shows the locations of treasures which are guarded by serpents. He has 30 legions under him.

# Ducax
He is a great **marquess** <term>Marquess: A high-ranking spirit in the infernal hierarchy, usually associated with high-level functions.</term> and appears in the likeness of a man, though he has the wings of a woman. He is seen favorably and invisibly. He grants mutual love, and especially that of widows. He grants the grace of languages <note>original: "gratia ligwarum" — likely *linguarum* (languages/tongues).</note> to the fullest. He causes men to cross from region to region with the swiftest speed. He has 22 legions under him. 

### Here begin the good, proven,
### and most true experiments;
### first, concerning the manner
### of writing Bel and Ebal and...

At the hour of the fifth day <term>Fifth day: Thursday (Feria Quinta), traditionally associated with the planet Jupiter and rituals of power or gain.</term>, clothed in new garments and having bathed, in the twilight, write your name upon a rib <note>original: "costam" — typically a rib bone of an animal used as a surface for the inscription.</note> in three parts: **Bel** and **Ebal**. Write "Bel" at the beginning, your name in the middle, and the name "Ebal" at the end of the three parts. Place it in the fire and let the rib be burned; then the soul [of the target] shall be drawn most strongly, as if by a fury. Read the invocation which follows, and you shall have what you desire.

# Conjuration of the Day of Jupiter

### To See and Know the Seven Seals

There follows the seeing and knowing of the seven seals which are to be marked in any work according to the **seven days** <note>original: "dieron septias"; likely a corruption of the Greek "heptas" for seven</note>, and these are the seals of the **seven planets** running through the seven heavens. Concerning this, the **seven wise philosophers** said that whoever performs a work, if he does not know [this], it will not be completed without these following signs. For each day has its own seal, its own planet, its own angels, and its own king with his assistants, its own creature, and its own **incense** <note>original: "suffumigium"; the ritual burning of herbs and resins to attract or compel spirits</note>, as will be told to you more specifically below. And when you do this, know that it was by the **Holy Spirit** and the great [man] who composed this book; and know that by whatever you work, let it benefit and be completed by the power of God, whose name be blessed forever and ever <note>original: "per infinita secula seculorum"</note>. Amen.

### On whatever day of the week
It has been said concerning all the days of the week in which one should work.

---

<margin>¶</margin> On whatever day of the week you wish to work for goodwill or any good or evil, pay diligent attention that the work is performed, so that you do not dismiss your work from day to day or delay it. And on the **Day of Mars** <note>Tuesday; original: "Diei martis"</note> three preside, namely: **Satiyel**, **Samyel**, and **Baniama**.

<meta>This page continues from the discussion of the angels governing the days of the week and the planetary hours. While the previous page listed the presiding spirits for Wednesday through Saturday, this section transitions into the specific magical properties and secret names of the individual hours of the day, detailing the types of "charters" or talismans that should be created during each specific time.</meta>

Therefore, the **first hour** of the day is called **Yayn**. In this hour, a parchment <note>original: "carta"; refers to a ritual document or scroll, often made of virgin vellum</note> is to be made for catching the voices of men and their evil words, and for binding <term>binding: a common magical practice (ligatio) intended to restrain or silence an opponent or a physical beast</term> their tongues.

For the **second hour** of the day is called **Yayn-Ou**. In it, one may work toward gaining favor and obtaining benefits, and for being admitted into the company of men, and to kings and to powerful people.

But the **third hour** of the day is called **Nassura**; in it, you should make a parchment or images <note>original: "ymagines"; likely referring to astrological talismans or effigies designed to represent a target</note> for hunting wild beasts or birds, or for catching <gloss>entangling</gloss> fish, as well as for those things for which growth <gloss>that which is being born</gloss> must be prevented.

The **fourth hour** of the day is called **Galaan**; in it, make a parchment or an image to bind all wild beasts—lions, bears, and the like.

The **fifth hour** of the day is called **Sadadat**; in it, make parchments for binding and for drawing near <gloss>applying</gloss> any beasts whatsoever, and for that which serves no other purpose.

The **sixth hour** of the day is called **Tamhynt**, in which you will be able to work toward extracting captives from prison and releasing those who are bound.

The **seventh hour** of the day is called **Caroumea**; it is used for establishing peace between kings.

The **eighth hour** of the day is called **Thralmea**; you will be able to work toward friendship and [resolving] discord...

The **tenth hour** of the day is called **Hyan**. My work in this hour is powerful against demons, or those possessed by demons <term>demoniacos: individuals believed to be under the influence of malevolent spirits</term>, or against an evil wind, or fear, or for visions <note>original: "uiedū", likely "visum"; referring to ghostly apparitions</note> seen by all.

<margin>C</margin> When you wish to know the proper caution <note>original: "cautelam"; refers to the necessary precautions or ritual procedures</note> for any operations, practitioners should know the hours and the names of the **Angels of the hours**, and who rules over them, so that at the opportune time they may be called upon by name. These are the names of the Angels ruling over all hours of the day and night.

In the **first hour** of the day, **Raphael** rules; in the **2nd hour**, **Anael**; in the **3rd**, **Michael**; in the **4th**, **Gabriel**; in the **5th**, **Cassiel**; in the **6th**, **Sarquiel**; in the **7th**, **Samael**; in the **8th**, **Raphael**; in the **9th**, **Anael**; in the **10th**, **Michael**; in the **eleventh**, **Gabriel**; and in the **12th**, **Cassiel**. 

And for the **night of Monday** <note>original: "diei lune"; literally "of the day of the moon," referring to Monday or the moon's planetary day</note>, in the **first hour** **Sarquiel** rules; in the **2nd hour**, **Samael**; in the **3rd**, **Raphael**; in the **4th**, **Anael**; in the **5th**, **Michael**; in the **6th**, **Gabriel**; in the **7th**, **Cassiel**; in the **8th**, **Sarquiel**; in the **9th**, **Samael**; in the **10th**, **Raphael**; in the **11th**, **Anael**; and in the **12th**, **Michael**. 

You ought to understand the notation for all other nights and days in this same way, for indeed certain angels rule and have command in the lordship of the planets <term>lordship of the planets: the "dominatio planetarum" refers to the traditional astrological belief that specific celestial bodies govern specific times and earthly affairs</term>, so that they command these matters. 

If the **hour of the Sun** <note>Sunday</note> commands, then **Raphael** rules.
The **hour of Venus**, **Anael**.
The **hour of Mercury**, **Michael**.
The **hour of the Moon**, **Gabriel**.
The **hour of Saturn**, **Cassiel**.
The **hour of Jupiter**, **Sarquiel**.
The **hour of Mars**, **Samael**.

On the day and [under] whichever planet, with its angels and the signs of the planets distributed, one must remember the names of these angels and they shall respond.

On the works of images <note>original: "operibus ymaginum"</note>... against evil tongues, the ritual must be performed at the first hour of the day. 

An image for binding tongues; and once bound at that hour, the one who was bound shall never be able to speak evil against you. At the first hour of the day, make an image out of wax, engraved like a sparrow <note>original: "passer"; birds were common symbols in such magic to represent the "flight" of words or the voice</note> with pure gold, and carve into the head of the image the name of the person you are working upon, and the name of the Lord of the hour and the day on which you do this; and out of reverence for the image, [carve] the names of the most powerful and highest Lord. You shall fumigate the image with the incense mentioned above and with red blood, and you shall wrap it in a new white cloth and bury it in the direction of the person you desire, and they shall not speak evil of you. Thus shall the mouths of all the joints of men <note>original: "articula hominum"; this likely refers to the "joints" or mechanical parts of speech, meaning all ability to articulate words against the practitioner</note> be bound, and they shall never be able to harm you as long as this image remains.

At the **sixth hour of the day**, an image <note>original: "ymago"; here referring to a magical figurine or talisman</note> of benevolence must be made: one image of a man and another of a woman made from the **purest cast tin** <note>In traditional alchemy and astrology, tin is the metal of Jupiter, the planet associated with benevolence, growth, and friendship</note>. In the head of the [woman's] image, carve the name of the man, and in the head of the man, carve the name of the woman. In the breast of both images, you shall carve the seven following names; let these names be the same as the seven names mentioned above <note>likely referring to a list of angelic or planetary names provided earlier in the manuscript</note>. 

Join the images together by gluing them with **wax** so that they are firmly fixed to one another, and bury them in the house of the person whom you wish to follow the other <note>i.e., to ensure the target of the spell remains loyal or devoted</note>. Lasting friendship shall be created between them for as long as the images endure. And these are the seven names themselves that you shall write: **Malchayl, Carmael, Sezehiel, Zarmayl, Raylail, Porbayl, Sezalabelis**; and these are the names that were read.

At the **seventh hour of the day**, an image must be made to summon <note>original: "p̃uocandas" (provocandas); to call forth or attract</note> all birds and all kinds of fish to a designated place. In this hour, fashion an image out of **red wax** in the likeness of the animal you wish to attract. Carve into the head of the image the name of the **Lord of the hour** <note>Each hour of the day was believed to be governed by a specific planetary spirit or angel</note> and do the same upon the breast. 

Also [carve] the seven true names of the highest hour, and fumigate <note>The practice of "suffumigation" involved passing the object through the smoke of specific burning herbs to consecrate it</note> the image with **fennel** and **barley**. Then, bury it in the location where you wish the animals to gather—the animals whose likeness you used to form the image. You shall then see the wonders of the Creator, and how they will come to that very place.

The seven images are now finished, which are to be made in the similar hours of the day with all their auxiliary [prayers], lords, and works. Therefore, take care that you fail in none of these, because they are of charity and truth; do not let the results lie, for through them you shall fulfill your whole will in the grace of God, whose name be blessed now and in the works of the images of the night.

¶ Now I shall describe the operation of the images of the night, just as the ancients handed them down, which are noted [here], and the manner in which they worked them so that you may make them in all the appropriate hours. Provide a lamp that can never be extinguished.

---

### The **first hour of the night**
An image <term>ymago: a ritual figurine or talisman</term> must be made for illuminating dark places, so that for those [who enter] an obscure place [it might be lit]. In this hour, cast from mud a vessel like a crucible <term>cruciabulum: a small lamp or melting pot</term>. [Take] these seven names and carve them upon the edge of the crucible: **Noctam, Saraptam, Haras, Dalas, Sassa, and Poe**. And at each edge of the crucible, [place] a wick of cotton <term>bombace: cotton, derived from the Latin 'bombax'</term> and write upon the mouth of the crucible these twelve names: **Bezar, Barith, Zamin, Calpam, Dalfar, Dana, Anath, Beas, Olamith, Hassas, Dalaph, Sasa**. And make from [the mud] above the crucible an image of a man holding a torch in the form of a vessel, in the likeness of one pouring oil into a candle. Fill the candle with oil that has not been pressed by hands <note>original: "non fuit expressum manibus"; this likely refers to 'virgin' oil or oil produced through a specific ritual purity process rather than common manual labor</note>. And carve upon the face of the image: **Ypud**.

### In the **fourth hour of the night**
An image must be made for a village or place to be destroyed, whatever you wish of the properties and the enemy. In this hour, urinate yourself, or [collect] the "dew" <note>original: "rore"; a common euphemism in alchemical and magical texts for animal urine or specialized fluids</note> of a camel or a cat, and collect it in a cloth of that hour. ### In the **fifth hour of the night**
An image must be made to scatter an army gathered over a castle or any other place by means of evil clouds <note>original: "malas nubes"; likely referring to dark omens or literal storm-clouds summoned by magic</note>. And carve into it these names: **BawaA**, **Ade**, **Hace**, **Centbawa**, **Canngla**, **Te**, **Ha**, **Coadimes**, **Cemal**, **Alul**, **Beaah**, **Aguel**. And above [write]: **Nafaca**, **Valuo**. 

Then fumigate <term>suffūga: the ritual act of passing an object through smoke or incense</term> the image with the fumigations which we have taught, and call upon and invoke the seven names written upon it. Bury that image in a high place and also in the public square <note>original: "in fori"; referring to the forum or marketplace, the civic center</note>.

<margin>¶</margin> Take one choice <term>boy</term> <note>original: "puerum." In medieval magic, young, "pure" children were believed to be more sensitive to spiritual visions.</note> and make three circles. At the first circle, let it be written: "In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit." At the second, say the psalm **The heavens declare** <note>original: "caeli enarrant," referring to Psalm 19 (Psalm 18 in the Vulgate).</note>. At the third, say **Give the king your judgment, O God** <note>original: "Deus iudicium tuum regi da," referring to Psalm 72 (Psalm 71 in the Vulgate).</note>. After these things, take that same foot and under it write the name of the boy, and under the boy’s feet let this name be written: **Tatras Vamaron**. And let two stones be placed on top so that the boy does not touch <note>The text implies the boy should not touch the bare ground, maintaining ritual purity.</note> and the name [is] under the feet. 

Afterward, write upon the handle of the **knife** <term>cultelli</term> both the name of the boy and these names: **Rach**, **Hoag**, **Abays**, **Papach**; and on the thumb write **Alphazo**. Then, anoint the nail of the right thumb with the knife, provided the handle is made of black or white horn. Then say: "Lord, [have mercy on me] a sinner" <note>original: "Domine pro me peccatore."</note>. 

Afterward, make a cross ✠ even upon the forehead of the boy, saying: "You who are **Alpha and Omega** <note>original: "Alpha et O." A title for Christ as the beginning and the end.</note>, make this boy speak <insert>the truth</insert> about the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, who is your Creator." In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and say: "Lord Jesus, Son of the living God, who for us sinners descended from the bosom of the Father to the earth, show us the truth. Amen." 

Then, whisper to the boy into his right ear: **Rachon**, **Beelzebub**, **Astaroth**, **Berith**, **Azvoto**, **Rotuda**. Say these three times. Then move the circles and place [them] so that you may prepare the boy; then anoint the lines of the boy’s nose with olive oil, and give the knife into the boy’s hand and say: **Alvgo**, **Safut**, **Bos**, **Zelates**, **Um**, **Vom**, **Atmyo**, **Chrtodens**, **Gannrias**, **Gana**, **Vasus**, **Pharaohta**, **Foliach**, **Gebach**, **Dyag**.

<page-num>103</page-num>

<image-desc>A circular diagram at the top of the page consists of four hand-drawn concentric circles. Three lines of Latin text are written along the inner curves of the top half of the diagram.</image-desc>

> In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "In noīe pr̄is & filij & sp̄s scti"</note>
> The heavens declare [the glory of God] <note>original: "Celi enarrant"; a reference to the opening of Psalm 19.</note>
> God, give your kingdom [to the king] <note>original: "Deus tuum regū da"; likely a shorthand reference to Psalm 72:1, *Deus, judicium tuum regi da* (Give the king your justice, O God).</note>

---

-> A true and proven **experiment** <term>Experiment: In medieval grimoires, this term refers to a specific magical procedure or "recipe" that has been tested.</term> concerning **theft** <note>original: "furto"</note> <-

<margin>☽</margin> <note>The symbol for the Moon, suggesting the ritual's connection to lunar timing or planetary influence.</note>
At the rising of the sun, make 3 circles and a cross in the middle of the circles. ### Ritual for the Magical Horse

Likewise, to obtain this <note>The text likely refers to the means of travel to the treasure previously discussed.</note>, one should write these things on the door of the house at the twilight of the night in the Hebrew manner <note>This likely instructs the practitioner to write from right to left, or to use Hebrew characters.</note>, along with these names and the botanical signs handed down: 

Serahia, Alpha, Draco, Hamodo, Ypanon.

Once these are written, withdraw a little distance from the place. After one hour, return and you will see a horse prepared. When you wish to mount it, place your left foot in the stirrup and say this conjuration:

> I conjure you, O horse, by the creator of heaven and earth, and by him who created all things for the praise and glory of his name, and by the living God, and by the holy God, and by all [divine powers], that you may not have the power to truly harm me—neither in body, nor in spirit, nor in my smallest limb—nor disturb me in any way, but that you carry me, <insert>Name</insert>, to such a place calmly, cheerfully, joyfully, and swiftly without any hindrance. 

Then mount boldly and securely.

### The Lunar Course of Days
Some are good and others are bad for writing charms. <term>cartas: literally "papers" or "cards," referring here to the parchment or paper used to write down magical seals and spells.</term>

The first day of the month, namely the lunar month, is entirely good. On the second day, the morning is good and no more. On the third day, you ought to do nothing. The fourth day is entirely good. The fifth day is good until the third hour, but not beyond that. On the sixth day, you shall do nothing. On the seventh day, the first hour is good and no more. On the eighth day, the morning is good. On the ninth day, you shall do nothing. On the tenth day, you should do nothing at all, because it is very bad for doing all things. The eleventh day is entirely good. The twelfth day is good until the third hour, like the fourth. On the 13th, you shall do nothing. The 14th is entirely good. On the 15th, likewise, you shall do nothing. On the 16th, the morning is entirely good, but afterward it is worth nothing. On the 17th and 18th, it is bad until midday, and afterward good. On the 19th, you shall do nothing then. On the 20th, 21st, and 22nd...

<summary>This page concludes an invisibility ritual involving a dove and a mud pellet, then provides a detailed lunar calendar (hemerology) specifying which days of the moon are auspicious or dangerous for creating magical charms.</summary>
<keywords>invisibility, dove ritual, sigils, hemerology, lunar calendar, magical timing, charms</keywords>

--- Page 218 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Lunar Course of Days" (hemerology) started on the previous page, detailing the suitability of specific days for magical or medical work. It then shifts abruptly into alchemical and pharmaceutical recipes for dissolving silver and creating a composite substance involving various resins, salts, and "toad powder."</meta>

The morning hours are good and no more for days 23 and 24.
On the 25th: do nothing; it is neither bad nor good.
On the 26th and 27th: do nothing.
The 28th, 29th, and 30th are entirely good for all things.

### On the Preparation of Silver and Mercury

From another <term>vitriol stone: a sulfate mineral, often used in alchemy to create acids</term>, grind the red portion thoroughly and make <term>aqua fortis: literally "strong water," refers to nitric acid</term> from them. Dissolve in it a limited amount of <term>silver: original "lune," the alchemical name for silver</term>, as much as it is able to dissolve. And <gloss>when</gloss> it can dissolve no more silver, then take that water in which the silver has been dissolved and add as much raw <term>mercury: or quicksilver</term> as can be thickened <note>original: "coagulare," to solidify or thicken a liquid into a mass</note>. Likewise, take the substance thickened in this way and place it in a <term>crucible: a heat-resistant ceramic container used for melting metals</term> over burning coals so that the living part <note>original: "pars vivu," possibly referring to the "living" or volatile mercury</note>...

Take: new wax, white frankincense, gum arabic, <term>tragacanth: a natural gum derived from dried sap</term>, <term>asafoetida: a pungent herbal resin</term>, <term>sagapenum: a yellow-brown resin from a Persian plant</term>, borax, gum arabic, <term>cantharides: "Spanish fly," often used in medicine and alchemy</term>, <term>toad powder: original "puluis buffonu"</term>, <term>ceruse: white lead</term>, saltpeter, common salt <note>original: "sal omne," likely meaning salt of any kind or common salt</note>, glass, turpentine, and <term>euphorbium: a caustic resin from the spurge plant</term> with equal parts of honey. Prepare it so that nothing will flow away and...

Toad powder is the copper that is scaled like rust <note>or verdigris</note> when it is melted.

### Likewise, a prayer to Jesus the Redeemer

**Likewise**, a prayer to Jesus the Redeemer, who came into this world: save me, most merciful Father. Help me; into your hands, O Lord, I commend my spirit <note>original: "spiritum"</note>, my soul, my heart, my body, and all my limbs.
